# Take over the sxr CLI refactor in reviewed slices

You are the implementation agent. Take ownership of the sxr command review below and turn it into a staged migration. The originating Codex agent will review your work. Start by establishing the current baseline, grouping the report into tasks, and implementing the first bounded `prompts` slice. Deliver that slice for review before starting the next one. Carry the work through implementation and verification within that slice; a plan alone does not complete this handoff.

Work in `/path/to/sxr`. This handoff was prepared on 2026-09-11. All paths below are relative to that repository unless absolute.

The user's complaint is concrete: `sxr prompts --codex @2 --all` included material that was not human prompts, and requiring `-n 0 --budget 0` to retrieve complete prompts is poor CLI behavior. The user then requested a review of every command and flag before more implementation. That review is complete. Your job is to make the work manageable and implement it in reviewable increments.

- `tests/test_prompt_defaults.py` and `audit/2026-09-10/remediation/prompt-defaults-before.log`

Use current source and reproducible observations to verify the report. Its `after`, `example_after`, priorities, and proposed-option rows are proposals. Document disagreements or changed source instead of implementing every suggestion mechanically. Preserve the original report and historical evidence; put new work under a new execution directory.

## Establish the baseline before editing

At handoff, HEAD is `48b11c6cf08200de363e108924e42fdbc52d83e8` and the package version is 0.12.2. The working tree contains substantial tracked and untracked changes from earlier remediation. Capture the actual HEAD, status, tracked diff, and hashes of relevant tracked and untracked files. Preserve all existing work. A fresh worktree from HEAD alone would omit the current implementation baseline.

Earlier work resolved 17 audit findings and recorded 632 passing tests plus 67 passing CLI contracts. Those results predate the interrupted prompt work. The draft prompt test run recorded 8 failures and 2 passes; the proposed behavior was not implemented. Measure the current state and distinguish existing failures from regressions. A historical green run is not evidence that today's tree passes.

The installed `/opt/homebrew/bin/sxr` was not updated during that work. Exercise the checkout through the project's source invocation and test runner. Use synthetic transcripts and temporary cache roots for behavioral tests so live session data does not affect results.

## Turn the report into an execution queue

Create a short baseline note, a list of contracts to preserve, a task list, and a ledger under `audit/2026-09-11/cli-refactor/`. Choose another dated directory if execution begins later. Keep the documents concise enough to resume without re-reading this entire attachment.

Each task needs a stable ID, the affected CSV row IDs, exact before/after behavior, likely files, real dependencies, acceptance checks, and a review checkpoint. Map every CSV row to a task or an explicit disposition such as retain, duplicate of another contract, deferred, or decision needed. A deferred proposal remains visible as unfinished scope.

Separate existing defects, intentional behavior changes, and optional additions. The first implementation task is the prompt behavior below. Later candidates include parser placement consistency, session range handling, output limits and JSON contracts, and exact follow-up commands for cleaning. New aliases, extra JSON formats, and broad cache restructuring stay deferred until they have a concrete need and a reviewed design.

Size a task around one behavior or tightly related command family. Split tasks that require unrelated changes. Keep mechanical moves separate from intentional behavior changes. Extract shared code only when a concrete slice needs it, then migrate callers incrementally through the existing command boundaries.

## First slice: complete human prompts

Verify the relevant report rows and draft tests, then implement and document this behavior:

- Plain `sxr prompts` prints the complete human prompts selected by the command, without requiring `-n 0 --budget 0` or silently inheriting display truncation from budget environment defaults.
- `--all` controls completeness. It lifts output limits without changing which records qualify as human prompts.
- Broader transcript context is an explicit, separately named selection option. The report and draft tests propose `--include-context`; inspect the exact inclusion semantics and describe them in help and tests.
- Explicit row and character limits remain usable when the caller requests compact output. Make precedence with `--all` deliberate and tested.
- Preserve complete source records in raw JSON, including physical-record identity and deduplication where required by the existing contract.

Cover Claude and Codex with realistic synthetic provenance, injected instructions, compacted context, tool results, long human input, and relevant legacy records. Exercise root-prefixed and command-local options where supported. Check negative and zero limits, environment defaults, and explicit option precedence. Keep range-selection changes in a separate task if they would expand this slice substantially; record that boundary.

Treat the draft tests as proposed expectations to assess against the user requirement and source formats. If a test is wrong, explain the correction and preserve coverage of the actual requirement. Do not weaken assertions merely to get a green run.

## Migration and verification rules

The code already has boundaries in `cli.py`, `flags.py`, `scope_options.py`, `output.py`, and the command/view modules. Direct `find`, `skills`, and `serve` dispatch also uses argparse paths alongside Typer entry points. Shared changes must account for both paths, including root flag placement and startup behavior.

Preserve existing contracts unless the active task explicitly changes them: provider defaults, exact `--file` selection, raw-record JSON shapes, physical line references, exit codes and documented exceptions, and the prior audit fixes. Keep provider parsing, indexing, worker protocol, and secret-rewrite internals scoped to demonstrated needs. In particular, cleaning output limits must continue to affect reporting rather than the set of files processed.

Run focused tests for each behavior change and the affected command matrix for shared selection, output, or parser changes. Run relevant lint, formatting, and `konpy` checks. Use the full suite at integration checkpoints, and the existing bundle and relocation verification when entry points or packaging are affected. Record commands, exit codes, source identity, and evidence paths. Report unverified checks and environmental failures explicitly.

Follow `konpy.json`: production Python modules stay within 300 lines, test modules within 400, and public production modules, classes, and functions have docstrings. Follow the project's current tooling rather than adding a replacement test framework.

Inherited workflow instructions include using `rg` for searches and `rtk` for shell work (`rtk proxy` when exact output matters). Before re-deriving a past decision, search session history with distinctive clues. On this Mac, include both Claude profiles using `--claude-root ~/.claude --claude-root ~/.claude-work`. Follow any applicable instructions supplied to your session. Keep secrets out of output and use synthetic secret values in tests.

Keep this work local for review. Publishing, installation, release, real transcript rewrites, and sending messages through external services are outside this handoff. Preserve the user's existing changes when creating checkpoints.

## Deliver a review packet after the first slice

Provide the originating reviewer with:

1. The task ID, affected CSV IDs, and exact behavior changed, including a concrete before/after invocation.
2. A bounded diff against your recorded starting working tree. Include untracked files. Because HEAD predates existing remediation, a plain diff against HEAD does not isolate your contribution.
3. Source identity, changed-file list, exact verification commands, results, and evidence paths. Identify baseline failures, new failures, and checks not run separately.
4. Compatibility changes, remaining limitations, and any decision the reviewer needs to assess.
5. The updated ledger and the next proposed slice, including why it depends on this one, if it does.

End at this review checkpoint and wait for the originating reviewer's feedback before beginning another slice. Resolve review findings within the same task, rerun the affected checks, and update the evidence. After a slice is accepted, repeat the process for the next task. Report progress using completed and remaining tasks; completing the first slice does not complete the entire refactor.

The reviewer will assess the actual diff, source behavior, and test evidence. Your summary helps them navigate those artifacts and does not substitute for them.

"CMD-grep-typer","command","sxr grep","typer","public","sxr grep","","","Claude. All sessions in exact cwd. Smart-case regex. No row cap. Enforce JSON and deduplication contracts.","sxr grep -F release --codex --all","Bounded search summaries. Clear recovery to complete matches.","P1","--all and -n 0 changes need migration notes. "PAR-grep-typer-pattern","argument","sxr grep","typer","public","PATTERN","","STRING; optional","none","One smart-case regex positional, optional only when --regexp/-e supplies it. "PAR-find-typer-index","option","sxr find","typer","public","--index","","BOOL; optional","off","Without a query prepares ranked FTS/catalog data and exits 0 even with zero results.
